\displaystyle\sup_{|\gamma|=[s+\alpha]}\left\|\Lambda^{\{s+\alpha\}}\left(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}D^{\gamma}\mathbf{V}\right)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\sup_{|\gamma|=[s]}\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Lambda^{\{s\}}D^{\gamma}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{H^{1-\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\|\mathbf{V}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.

Plugging this estimate into (
3.63
) gives that for each
\{s\}\in[\alpha,1),

(3.65)

\begin{split}&\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\\
\leq&C\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sup_{|\gamma|=[s]}\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Lambda^{\{s\}}D^{\gamma}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{\dot{H}^{\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\|\mathbf{V}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.\end{split}

Inserting (
3.62
), (
3.64
) and (
3.65
) into (
3.61
), we readily have for each
s>0,

(3.66)

\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{H^{s+1-\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C.

Using estimate (
3.33
), we get from (
3.66
) that for each
s\in[0,2\alpha-1],

(3.67)

\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C_{\sigma}\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C,

which means that for each
s\in[0,2\alpha-1],

\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\sigma,\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\right).

Plugging (
3.67
) into (
3.66
) yields that for each
s\in[2\alpha-1,2(2\alpha-1)],

\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\sigma,\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\right).

By repeating this process, we can conclude that for each
s\geq 0,

(3.68)

\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\right\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\sigma,\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\right).

Lastly, we turn to estimate pressure. Since

-\Delta P=\Div\Div(\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}-\mathbf{F})

and the fact
\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\beta}
belongs to
A_{2}
class, we get by (
3.27
) and (
3.32
) that

\displaystyle\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}P\right\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle C\left\|\left(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}-\mathbf{F}\right)\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{V}\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\mathbf{F}\|_{H^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty.

This estimate together with (
3.68
) implies the estimate (
3.30
).

The proof of the estimate (
3.31
) is similar to the proof of (
3.30
), so we here omit it.
∎

Now we come back to show the second estimate
\rm(ii)
-(2) in Theorem
1.4
. Taking
\mathbf{U}_{0}\equiv 0
in Theorem
3.3
, we immediately have

(\mathbf{U},P)\in H^{s+\alpha}_{\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\beta}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\times H_{\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\beta}}^{s+\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3}).

Lastly, we show the weighted estimate in Theorem
1.8
. According to Theorem
3.3
, it suffice to show that for each
\alpha\in[5/6,1),

(3.69)

\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty,

and for
\alpha=1,

(3.70)

\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\right\|_{H^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty.

Since
\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}=-\mathbf{U}_{0}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}
and
\mathbf{U}_{0}(\mathbf{x})=e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}}\mathbf{u}_{0}(\mathbf{x})
with
\mathbf{u}_{0}(\mathbf{x})=\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{x})}{|\mathbf{x}|^{2\alpha-1}}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\right\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{-1}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\bigg(\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{2ks}\big\|\dot{\Delta}_{k}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Since
\beta\in(0,\alpha)
and
\alpha\in[5/6,1]
, we have

4\alpha-(\beta+1)>\frac{3}{2}.

Moreover, one has by the Hölder inequality and Proposition 2.8 in
[
10
]
that

(3.71)

\begin{split}&\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\\
\leq&\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2\alpha-1}\mathbf{U}_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta-2\alpha+1}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\\
\leq&\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2\alpha-1}\mathbf{U}_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2\alpha}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta-4\alpha+1}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty.\end{split}

On the other hand, we see by the Bernstein inequality in Lemma
2.1
that

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{2ks}\big\|\dot{\Delta}_{k}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\bigg(\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{2k(s-[s]-1)}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}\sup_{|\gamma|=[s]+1}\big\|D^{\gamma}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{|\gamma|=[s]+1}\big\|D^{\gamma}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.

Thanks to the Leibniz formula, we obtain

\displaystyle\sup_{|\gamma|=[s]+1}\big\|D^{\gamma}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big)\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{|\gamma|=[s]+1}\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}D^{\gamma}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sum_{|\gamma_{1}|=1}^{[s]+1}\sup_{|\gamma_{1}|+|\gamma_{2}|=[s]+1}\big\|\partial^{\gamma_{1}}\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\partial^{\gamma_{2}}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.

Since
\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}=-\mathbf{U}_{0}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}
, we obtain by Proposition 2.8 in
[
10
]
that

\displaystyle\sup_{|\gamma|=[s]+1}\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}D^{\gamma}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{|\gamma_{1}|=1}^{[s]+1}\sup_{|\gamma_{1}|+|\gamma_{2}|=[s]+1}\big\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\partial^{\gamma_{1}}\mathbf{U}_{0}\partial^{\gamma_{2}}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{|\gamma_{1}|=1}^{[s]+1}\sup_{|\gamma_{1}|+|\gamma_{2}|=[s]+1}\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2\alpha-1+|\gamma_{1}|}\partial^{\gamma_{1}}\mathbf{U}_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{2\alpha+|\gamma_{2}|}\partial^{\gamma_{2}}\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\times

\displaystyle\times\left\|\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta-4\alpha-[s]}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty.

By the Hölder inequality, we get

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma_{1}|=1}^{[s]+1}\sup_{|\gamma_{1}|+|\gamma_{2}|=[s]+1}\big\|\partial^{\gamma_{1}}\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\partial^{\gamma_{2}}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sum_{|\gamma_{1}|=1}^{[s]+1}\sup_{|\gamma_{1}|+|\gamma_{2}|=[s]+1}\big\|\partial^{\gamma_{1}}\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\big\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\big\|\partial^{\gamma_{2}}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\big\|\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.

Since
\alpha\in[5/6,1],
we have by the Leibniz estimate, (
3.27
) and (
3.28
) that

\displaystyle\big\|\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle C\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{H^{s}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{2}\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{W^{s,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty.

So, we have

\bigg(\sum_{k\geq 0}2^{2ks}\big\|\dot{\Delta}_{k}\big(\langle\cdot\rangle^{\beta}\Div\mathbf{F}_{0}\big)\big\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\bigg)^{\frac{1}{2}}<\infty.

This estimate together with (
3.71
) yields the claim (
3.69
). Next, by the same argument used in the proof of (
3.69
), we can show the claim (
3.70
). So, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.8
.

4.
The optimal decay estimate for the forward self-similar solutions to the generalized Navier-Stokes equations

In this section, we are going to show the optimal decay estimate for the forward self-similar solutions to the generalized Navier-Stokes equations. Firstly, the existence of the large forward self-similar solutions

(4.1)

\mathbf{u}=t^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}\mathbf{U}_{0}(\mathbf{x}/t^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})+t^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{x}/t^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})

to the three-dimensional generalized Navier-Stokes system with
\alpha\in(5/8,1]
was shown in
[
10
]
, where the homogeneous part
\mathbf{U}_{0}=\frac{\sigma(\mathbf{x})}{|\mathbf{x}|^{2\alpha-1}}
with
\sigma(\mathbf{x})=\sigma(\mathbf{x}/|\mathbf{x}|)\in C^{1,0}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, and the inhomogeneous part
\mathbf{V}
solves

(4.2)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &(-\Delta)^{\alpha}\mathbf{V}-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}\mathbf{V}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\mathbf{x}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{V}+\nabla P=-\mathbf{V}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{V}+L_{\mathbf{U}_{0}}(\mathbf{V})-\mathbf{U}_{0}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0},\\
&\textnormal{div}\,\mathbf{V}=0,\end{aligned}\ \right.

with

L_{\mathbf{U}_{0}}\mathbf{V}=-\mathbf{U}_{0}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{V}-\mathbf{V}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}.

Since
\sigma\in C^{1,0}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, we have by Theorem
3.1
that for each
2\leq p<\infty,

(4.3)

\|\mathbf{V}\|_{B^{2\alpha}_{p,p}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\left(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\right).

Thus, our task is devoted to showing the optimal decay estimate for the solutions
\mathbf{V}
to the problem (
4.2
). To do this, we firstly construct the approximate solutions
(\mathbf{V}_{k},\,P_{k})
to the corresponding linearized equations

(4.4)

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &(-\Delta)^{\alpha}\mathbf{V}_{k}-\frac{2\alpha-1}{2\alpha}\mathbf{V}_{k}-\frac{1}{2\alpha}\mathbf{x}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}+\nabla P_{k}=\mathbf{f}_{k},\\
&\textnormal{div}\,\mathbf{V}_{k}=0,\end{aligned}\ \right.

where

\mathbf{f}_{k}(\mathbf{x})=-\phi_{k}(\mathbf{x})\Big(\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{V}_{k}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}_{k}+\mathbf{V}_{k}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}\Big)(\mathbf{x})

and
\phi_{k}=\phi(\mathbf{x}/k)
with the smooth cut-off function
\phi
satisfying

\phi(\mathbf{x})=\begin{cases}1\quad&\mathbf{x}\in B_{\frac{R}{2}}(0),\\
0\quad&\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{R}(0).\end{cases}

By the standard degree theory as used in
[
10
]
, it is easy to prove that Cauchy problem (
4.4
) admits one weak solutions
\mathbf{V}_{k}\in H^{\alpha}_{\sigma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
such that

\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{H^{\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C(\|\sigma_{0}\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

Since
\operatorname{supp}\mathbf{f}_{k}\subset B_{k}(0)
, we can write the weak solutions
\mathbf{V}_{k}
in the integral form in terms of Proposition 4.1 established in
[
11
]
that

(4.5)

\mathbf{V}_{k}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{0}^{1}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}(1-s)}\mathbb{P}{\rm div}_{\mathbf{x}}\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\mathbf{f}_{k}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,{\rm d}s.

Next, we are going to show the optimal decay estimate for the
\mathbf{V}_{k}
in two steps, which implies the optimal decay estimate for
\mathbf{V}
by taking
k\to\infty
. Let us begin with the lower decay estimate for
\mathbf{V}.

4.1.
The lower decay estimate

In this subsection, we proceed with the lower decay estimate for the
\mathbf{V}_{k}
by using boundedness of the operator
\nabla\mathbb{P}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}s}
in the weighted space. Specifically,

Proposition 4.1
.

Let

(4.6)

\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{0}^{1}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}(1-s)}\mathbb{P}{\rm div}_{\mathbf{x}}\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\mathbf{f}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,{\rm d}s.

(i)

If
\alpha\in[5/6,1]
and
\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\in L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
satisfies

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|<\infty,

we have

(4.7)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{W}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right).

(ii)

If
\alpha\in[5/6,1)
and
\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap\dot{W}^{1,6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
satisfies

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|<\infty,

we have

(4.8)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-1}|\nabla\otimes\mathbf{W}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right).

(iii)

If
\alpha=1
and
\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\in\dot{W}^{1,\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap\dot{W}^{1,6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
satisfies

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{3}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|<\infty,

we have

(4.9)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{3}|\nabla\otimes\mathbf{W}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{3}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right).

Proof.

From (
4.6
), we know that

\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\nabla\big(O_{\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big)\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s,

where
O_{\alpha}
is the generalized Oseen kernel of operator
\mathbb{P}G_{\alpha}
with
G_{\alpha}=e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}s}
.

Since
\alpha\in[5/6,1]
and
\mathbf{f}\in L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3}),
we have by the Young inequality that

(4.10)

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{W}|(\mathbf{x})\leq&\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\|(\nabla O_{\alpha})(1-s,\cdot)\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\mathbf{f}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&C\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)^{-\frac{3}{4\alpha}}s^{\frac{5}{4\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\left\|\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.\end{split}

Noting that
O_{q,\alpha}=\dot{\Delta}_{q}O_{\alpha}
, we rewrite
\mathbf{W}
as

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\nabla O_{\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\cdot\mathbf{f}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\,s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{q<0}+\sum_{q\geq 0}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\nabla O_{q,\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\cdot\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\,s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{\natural}+\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}.

We first proceed to bound
\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}
, which can be decomposed into two parts

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}=

\displaystyle\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\bigg)\nabla O_{q,\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\cdot\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\,s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}_{1}+\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}_{2}.

Since
\alpha\in[5/6,1]
, we have

(4.11)

\begin{split}\big|\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}_{2}\big|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\nabla O_{q,\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})|\,\left||\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}\right|\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\,s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}2^{q}e^{-c2^{2q\alpha}(1-s)}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}.\end{split}

For the term
\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}_{1}
, we see that

\displaystyle\big|\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}_{1}\big|\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|^{3}|\nabla O_{q,\alpha}|(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\cdot\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\,s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s.

By the Young inequality and Lemma
2.8
, we have

(4.12)

\begin{split}\big|\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}_{1}\big|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}2^{q}e^{-c2^{2q\alpha}(1-s)}\,\mathrm{d}s\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}.\end{split}

Now we turn to bound
\mathbf{V}^{\natural}
. Firstly, one writes

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{\natural}=

\displaystyle\sum_{q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\bigg)\nabla O_{q,\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\cdot\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\,s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{\natural}_{1}+\mathbf{W}^{\natural}_{2}.

Similar to the proof of (
4.11
), we can show that

(4.13)

\begin{split}\big|\mathbf{W}^{\natural}_{2}\big|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}\sum_{q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}|\nabla O_{q,\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})|\times\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad\quad\times\left||\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}\right|\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\,s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}\sum_{q<0}2^{q}\int_{0}^{1}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}.\end{split}

Lastly, we obtain

(4.14)

\begin{split}\big|\mathbf{W}^{\natural}_{1}\big|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\sum_{q<0}2^{q}\int_{0}^{1}\,\mathrm{d}s\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}.\end{split}

Collecting estimates (
4.11
), (
4.12
), (
4.13
) and (
4.14
), we readily have

\sup_{|\mathbf{x}|\geq 1}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{W}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right).

This inequality together with (
4.10
) leads to

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{W}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x}),

which implies the first desired estimate in Proposition
4.1
.

Next, we turn to show decay estimate for
\nabla\otimes\mathbf{W}.
Taking derivative of
\mathbf{W}
with respect to
x_{k}
, it follows from (
4.6
) that
\forall\,k\in\{1,2,3\},

(4.15)

\partial_{x_{k}}\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\partial_{x_{k}}\big(O_{\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big){\rm div}\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s.

By the Young inequality, one has

(4.16)

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left|\partial_{x_{k}}\mathbf{W}\right|(\mathbf{x})\leq&\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\partial_{x_{k}}\big(O_{\alpha}(1-s,\cdot)\big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&+\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{1}\left\|\partial_{x_{k}}\big(O_{\alpha}(1-s,\cdot)\big)\right\|_{L^{\frac{6}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&C\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{2}}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}s^{\frac{5}{4\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s+C\int_{\frac{1}{2}}^{1}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}(1-s)^{-\frac{3}{4\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&C\left(\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\right).\end{split}

According to (
4.15
), we decompose
\mathbf{W}
into the following two parts

\displaystyle\partial_{x_{k}}\mathbf{W}=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}\bigg(\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}+\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\bigg)\partial_{x_{k}}\big(O_{\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big)\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{kS}+\mathbf{W}_{kL}.

Noting that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{W}_{kL}\right|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\big|\partial_{x_{k}}\big(O_{\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big)\big||\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s,

we get by the Young inequality that

(4.17)

\begin{split}\left|\mathbf{W}_{kL}\right|(\mathbf{x})\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\|\partial_{x_{k}}O_{\alpha}(1,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\int_{0}^{1}(1-s)^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x}),\end{split}

in the last line of (
4.17
), we have used the fact that
\partial_{x_{k}}O_{\alpha}(1,\mathbf{x})\in L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
. Indeed, we have by the Bernstein inequality in Lemma
2.1
that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x_{k}}O_{\alpha}(1,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{q}\|\dot{\Delta}_{q}\mathbb{P}G_{\alpha}(1,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{q\leq 0}2^{q}\|\dot{\Delta}_{q}G_{\alpha}(1,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sum_{q>0}2^{-q}\|D^{2}\dot{\Delta}_{q}G_{\alpha}(1,\cdot)\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|G_{\alpha}(1,\cdot)\|_{W^{2,1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty.

As for the term
\mathbf{W}_{kS},
we write in terms of the high-low decomposition

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{kS}=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{q\leq 0}+\sum_{q>0}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\partial_{x_{k}}\big(O_{q,\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big)\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\natural}+\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\sharp}.

For the high frequency regime, we easily find that

\displaystyle\left|\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\sharp}\right|\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}

\displaystyle|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|^{3}\big|\partial_{x_{k}}\big(O_{q,\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big)\big|\times

\displaystyle\times\big|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\big|s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s.

Moreover, we have by Lemma
2.8
and
\alpha\in[5/6,1)
that

(4.18)

\begin{split}\big|\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\sharp}\big|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\sum_{q\geq 0}2^{q}\int_{0}^{1}e^{-c2^{2q\alpha}(1-s)}\,\mathrm{d}s\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.\end{split}

For the low frequency regime, we see that

(4.19)

\begin{split}\big|\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\natural}\big|\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sum_{q\leq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}&|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|^{3}|\partial_{x_{k}}\left(O_{q,\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\right)|\times\\
&\times\left|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right|\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\,s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

Thus we have that for each
\alpha\in[5/6,1),

\displaystyle\big|\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\natural}\big|\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\sum_{q<0}2^{q}\int_{0}^{1}\,\mathrm{d}s\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\,\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.

Combining this estimate with (
4.16
), (
4.17
) and (
4.19
) yields the second desired estimate.

For
\alpha=1,
we apply the Young inequality and Lemma
2.8
to
\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\sharp}
to obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{q>0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|^{3}\big|\partial_{x_{k}}\big(O_{q,1}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\big)\big|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)s^{\frac{1}{2}-2}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sum_{q>0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left\||\cdot|^{3}\partial_{x_{k}}O_{q,1}(1-s,\cdot)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}s^{\frac{1}{2}-2}\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\sum_{q>0}2^{-\frac{q}{2}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\frac{3}{4}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})},

which implies that for
\alpha=1,

(4.20)

\big|\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\sharp}\big|(\mathbf{x})\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.

Similarly, we have from (
4.18
) that for
\alpha=1,

(4.21)

\begin{split}\big|\mathbf{W}_{kS}^{\natural}\big|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sum_{q\leq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\left\||\cdot|^{3}|\partial_{x_{k}}\left(O_{q,1}(1-s,\cdot)\right)|\right\|_{L^{5}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\times\\
&\qquad\qquad\qquad\times\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2}}\big)\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\,s^{\frac{1}{2}-2}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\sum_{q\leq 0}2^{q\frac{2}{5}}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\,\int_{0}^{1}s^{-\frac{3}{10}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{3}}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right\|_{L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.\end{split}

Collecting estimates (
4.16
), (
4.17
), (
4.20
) and (
4.21
) give the third desired estimate, and then we finish the proof of Proposition
4.1
.
∎

We apply Proposition
4.1
to
\mathbf{V}_{k}
to obtain

(4.22)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\right).

Since
\sigma\in C^{1,0}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, we get by using Theorem
3.1
that for all
p\in[2,\infty)

(4.23)

\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{B_{p,p}^{2\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

This estimate enables us to conclude by the Hölder inequality, (
3.27
) and (
3.28
) that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle C\left(\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\right)\|\nabla\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+C\left(\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\right)\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{H^{2\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}^{2}+C\|\mathbf{V}\|^{2}_{H^{2\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}^{2}+C\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

By the Hölder inequality, one has

(4.24)

\begin{split}&\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\\
\leq&R^{4\alpha-2}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{R}(0))}\sup_{|\mathbf{x}|\geq R}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x}),\end{split}

where
R
to be fixed later.

By the Hölder inequality, the interpolation inequality and Proposition 2.8 in
[
10
]
, we get

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}_{k}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}_{k}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\alpha-1}|\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\alpha-1}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})+\left(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\alpha-1}|\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})\right)^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\right)^{\frac{1}{2}}+C.

Moreover, we get by the Young inequality and
\sigma\in C^{1,0}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
that

(4.25)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}_{k}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}_{k}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C+\frac{1}{4}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x}).

Inserting (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) into (
4.22
) gives

(4.26)

\begin{split}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq&C+\frac{1}{4}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\\
&+\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{R}(0))}\sup_{|\mathbf{x}|\geq R}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x}).\end{split}

With the help of the interpolation theorem, we have

(4.27)

\begin{split}\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{R}(0))}\leq&\|(1-\phi_{R})\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\\
\leq&C\|(1-\phi_{R})\mathbf{V}\|^{\frac{1}{10}}_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\big\|\nabla\big((1-\phi_{R})\mathbf{V}\big)\big\|^{\frac{9}{10}}_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\\
\leq&C\|\mathbf{V}\|^{\frac{1}{10}}_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\big\|\nabla\big((1-\phi_{R})\mathbf{V}\big)\big\|^{\frac{9}{10}}_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.\end{split}

The Leibniz estimate allows us to conclude

\displaystyle\big\|\nabla\big((1-\phi_{R})\mathbf{V}\big)\big\|_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{R}\big\|\mathbf{V}\big\|_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\big\|\nabla\mathbf{V}\big\|_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{R}{2}(0)})}.

Plugging this estimate into (
4.27
), we readily have

(4.28)

\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{R}(0))}\leq\frac{C}{R^{\frac{9}{10}}}+C\big\|\nabla\mathbf{V}\big\|^{\frac{9}{10}}_{L^{\frac{18}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{R}{2}(0)})}.

Since
\mathbf{V}\in H^{2\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
with
\alpha\in[5/6,1]
, we get by the inclusion

H^{2\alpha}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\hookrightarrow\dot{W}^{1,\frac{18}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})

and (
4.28
) that

\lim_{R\to+\infty}\|\nabla\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{R}(0))}=0.

Thus, there exists a number
R_{0}>0
such that for all
R>R_{0}
,

\|\nabla\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{R}(0))}<\frac{1}{4}.

Then (
4.1
) becomes

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})})+\frac{1}{2}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x}),

which implies

(4.29)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

Since
\mathbf{V}
is the limit of
\mathbf{V}_{k}
, we get from Fatou’s lemma and (
4.29
) that

(4.30)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{V}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

Next, we begin to show the decay estimate for
\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}
which fulfills

\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\nabla O_{\alpha}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}){\rm div}_{\mathbf{x}}\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\mathbf{f}\big(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s.

Based on this equality, we readily have by Proposition
4.1
that for each
\alpha\in[5/6,1),

(4.31)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-1}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\right)

and for
\alpha=1

(4.32)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{3}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{3}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\right).

By the triangle inequality, (
3.27
), (
3.28
), (
4.3
) and (
4.30
), we easily find that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\right)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\mathbf{V}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}+\mathbf{V}_{k}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\mathbf{U}_{0}\cdot\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\alpha}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x}).

Since

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\alpha}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq\|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-1}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\right)^{\frac{2\alpha}{4\alpha-1}},

we have by the Young inequality that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\right)\leq

\displaystyle C+C\left(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{2\alpha-1}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\right)^{\frac{2\alpha}{4\alpha-1}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C+\frac{1}{4}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-1}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x}).

Inserting this estimate into (
4.31
) and (
4.32
), respectively, yields that for each
\alpha\in[5/6,1),

(4.33)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-1}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C

and for
\alpha=1
,

(4.34)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{3}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{\frac{4}{5}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+C.

By the Hölder inequality, (
3.27
), (
3.28
) and (
4.3
), one has that for each
\alpha\in[5/6,1],

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{6}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})})

and

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

Plugging both estimates into (
4.33
) gives that for each
\alpha\in[5/6,1),

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-1}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

For
\alpha=1,
we can show by the Hölder inequality (
3.27
), (
3.28
) and (
4.3
) again that

\displaystyle\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{k}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{4}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{V}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{2}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{10}{3}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\|\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{5}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\mathbf{U}_{0}\|_{L^{\frac{5}{3}}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

Inserting this estimate into (
4.34
) implies that for
\alpha=1,

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{3}|\nabla\mathbf{V}_{k}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

Finally, we get by taking
k\to\infty
that for each
\alpha\in[5/6,1],

(4.35)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-1}|\nabla\mathbf{V}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}).

4.2.
The decay estimate with logarithmic loss

In this subsection, we are going to show the almost optimal estimate with logarithmic loss which is caused by the Leray projector
\mathbb{P}
.

Proposition 4.2
.

Let
\alpha\in[5/6,1]
and
\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap W^{1,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
satisfies

(4.36)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\nabla\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|<\infty,

and

(4.37)

\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{0}^{1}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}(1-s)}\mathbb{P}{\rm div}_{\mathbf{x}}\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\mathbf{f}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,{\rm d}s.

Then
\mathbf{W}
enjoys the decay estimate

(4.38)

|\mathbf{W}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{-(4\alpha-1)}\log\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle\ \ \mbox{for each}\ \,\,\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}.

Proof.

Thanks to (
4.10
), it suffices to show the decay estimate of
\mathbf{W}
for large
|\mathbf{x}|.
So we always assume that
|\mathbf{x}|\geq 200
in the remaining part of the proof. In terms of the low-high frequency decomposition, one splits
\mathbf{W}
into three parts as follows

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{q\geq 0}+\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}+\sum_{q<N_{\mathbf{x}}}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\Delta}_{q}\mathbb{P}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}(1-s)}s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right)(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\rm I}+{\rm II}+{\rm III},

where
N_{\mathbf{x}}=-[\log_{2}|\mathbf{x}|]+1.

The first term
I
which can be rewritten as

\displaystyle{\rm I}=

\displaystyle\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{1}+{\rm I}_{2}.

The term
I_{2}
can be bounded as follows

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{2}=

\displaystyle\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}}\left(\frac{|\mathbf{y}|}{s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}\right)^{4\alpha-1}\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\big|O_{\alpha,q}\big|(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(\frac{|\mathbf{y}|}{s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}\right)^{4\alpha-1}\left|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s.

Lemma
2.8
enables us to conclude that

(4.39)

\begin{split}|{\rm I}_{2}|\leq&\frac{4}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}e^{-c(1-s)2^{2q\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\sum_{q\geq 0}2^{-2q\alpha}\leq C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.\end{split}

As for
{\rm I}_{1}
, we see that

\displaystyle{\rm I}_{1}=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle}{2}}(0)}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sum_{q\geq 0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}\big|O_{\alpha,q}\big|(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s.

Moreover, we have by Lemma
2.8
that

(4.40)

\begin{split}|{\rm I}_{1}|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sum_{q\geq 0}2^{-q(4\alpha-1-\frac{3}{2})}\int_{0}^{1}e^{-c(1-s)2^{2q\alpha}}s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&C\|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2+\frac{3}{4\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}s\leq C\|\mathbf{f}\|_{\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.\end{split}

For the term
{\rm II}
, we need to use the smooth cut-off function
\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})
to decompose the force
\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})
into two parts as follows

\displaystyle\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})=\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})+\left(1-\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})\right)\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})=:\mathbf{f}^{\natural}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})+\mathbf{f}^{\sharp}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}).

Thus, we can rewrite in terms of the support of
\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})
the second term
{\rm II}
as

(4.41)

\begin{split}{\rm II}=&\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\nabla O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\cdot\mathbf{f}^{\natural}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s\\
&+\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{4}}(0)}O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}^{\sharp}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s\\
=:&{\rm II}_{1}+{\rm II}_{2}.\end{split}

Following the same method as used in the proof of (
4.40
), we can show that
|{\rm II}_{1}|
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha+2}}\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha+2}\big|\nabla O_{\alpha,q}\big|(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\left|\mathbf{f}\right|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha+2}}\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}2^{-q(4\alpha+2-4)}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha+2}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha+2-4}|\mathbf{x}|^{3-(4\alpha-2)}\leq C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}},

which implies

(4.42)

|{\rm II}_{1}|\leq C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.

In the same fashion in (
4.39
), it is easy to show by the fact
|\nabla\phi_{\frac{\mathbf{x}}{2}}|\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|}
that

(4.43)

\begin{split}|{\rm II}_{2}|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{C\alpha-1}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|+\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\right)\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}e^{-c(1-s)2^{2q\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|+\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\right)\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}1\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left(\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|+\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\right)\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\log\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle.\end{split}

For the last term
{\rm III}
, we decompose it in terms of (
4.41
) that

\displaystyle{\rm III}=

\displaystyle\sum_{q<N_{\mathbf{x}}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\nabla O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\cdot\mathbf{f}^{\natural}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+\sum_{q<N_{\mathbf{x}}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{4}}(0)}\nabla O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\cdot\mathbf{f}^{\sharp}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\rm III}_{1}+{\rm III}_{2}.

We perform the process as used in deriving (
4.40
) to get that
|{\rm III}_{1}|
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sum_{q<N_{\mathbf{x}}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}\big|\nabla O_{\alpha,q}\big|(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\left|\mathbf{f}\right|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\sum_{q<N_{\mathbf{x}}}2^{-q(4\alpha-1-4)}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}}\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1-4}|\mathbf{x}|^{3-(4\alpha-2)}\leq C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.

This estimate guarantees that

(4.44)

|{\rm III}_{1}|\leq C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.

Now it remains to deal with
{\rm III}_{2}
. Since
|\mathbf{y}|\geq\frac{1}{4}|\mathbf{x}|
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm III}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{4}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}\sum_{q<N_{\mathbf{x}}}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}\big|\nabla O_{\alpha,q}\big|(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(\frac{|\mathbf{y}|}{s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}}\right)^{4\alpha-2}\left|\mathbf{f}\right|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}}\sum_{q<N_{\mathbf{x}}}2^{q}\leq C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})\right|\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.

Combining this estimate with (
4.39
), (
4.40
), (
4.42
), (
4.43
) and (
4.44
) yields

\sup_{|\mathbf{x}|\geq 2}|\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})|\leq C\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}\log\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle.

This inequality together with (
4.10
) implies the estimate (
4.38
).
∎

By Proposition
4.2
, we immediately get the decay estimate in Theorem
1.4
. Now we turn to show the decay estimate in Theorem
1.8
.
With both estimates (
4.33
) and (
4.35
) in hand, we write
\mathbf{V}
in the integral form in terms of Proposition 4.1 established in
[
11
]
that

(4.45)

\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{0}^{1}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}(1-s)}\mathbb{P}{\rm div}_{\mathbf{x}}\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\mathbf{f}\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,{\rm d}s,

where

\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})=-\Big(\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}\Big)(\mathbf{x}).

Since
\alpha\in[5/6,1]
and
\sigma\in C^{1,0}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, it is easy to verify by using both estimates (
4.33
) and (
4.35
) again that

\displaystyle\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle^{4\alpha-1}\operatorname{div}\Big(\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}\Big)(\mathbf{x})<\infty,

which meets the condition (
4.36
) in Proposition
4.2
. Thus we have

(4.46)

\left|\mathbf{V}(\mathbf{x})\right|\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})})(1+|\mathbf{x}|)^{4\alpha-1}\log^{-1}(1+|\mathbf{x}|),

which is the decay estimate in Theorem
1.8
.

4.3.
The optimal decay estimate

In this section, we are going to show the optimal decay estimate for
\mathbf{V}
with the special force
\mathbf{f}
. Since the decay rate for each term of
\mathbf{f}
is different, we decompose it into two parts as follows

\mathbf{f}(\mathbf{x})=-\Big(\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}\Big)(\mathbf{x})-\left(\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}\right)(\mathbf{x}),

where
\mathbf{f}_{1}=-\Big(\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{V}+\mathbf{V}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}\Big)(\mathbf{x}).

Proposition 4.3
.

Let
\alpha\in[\frac{5}{6},1]
,
\sigma\in C^{1,0}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
, and
\mathbf{V}
be a weak solution to the system (
1.6
).
Assume
\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x})\in\dot{H}^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})\cap W^{1,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
satisfies

(4.47)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x})\right|+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\nabla\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x})\right|<\infty,

and

(4.48)

\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})=\int_{0}^{1}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}(1-s)}\mathbb{P}{\rm div}_{\mathbf{x}}\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}(\mathbf{f}_{1}+\mathbf{f}_{2})\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,{\rm d}s.

If, moreover, there exists some
\gamma\in(0,1)
such that

(4.49)

\left\||\cdot|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty,

then we have

\left|\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})\right|\leq C\left(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})},\left\||\cdot|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\right)(1+|\mathbf{x}|)^{4\alpha-1}.

Proof.

From (
4.46
), we have

(4.50)

\left|\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})\right|\leq C(\|\sigma\|_{W^{1,\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})})(1+|\mathbf{x}|)^{4\alpha-1}\log^{-1}(1+|\mathbf{x}|).

This decay estimate allows us to write

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}(\mathbf{x})=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{1}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}(1-s)}\mathbb{P}{\rm div}_{\mathbf{x}}\left(s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\left(\mathbf{f}_{1}+\mathbf{f}_{2}\right)\big(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\big)\right)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{\natural}+\mathbf{W}^{\sharp}.

For
|\mathbf{x}|\geq 200,
as in the proof of Proposition
4.2
, we decompose
\mathbf{W}^{\natural}
as follows

\displaystyle\mathbf{W}^{\natural}=

\displaystyle\left(\sum_{q\geq 0}+\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}+\sum_{q<N_{\mathbf{x}}}\right)\int_{0}^{1}\dot{\Delta}_{q}\mathbb{P}e^{-(-\Delta)^{\alpha}(1-s)}s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}\right)(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\rm I}+{\rm II}+{\rm III},

where
N_{\mathbf{x}}=-[\log_{2}|\mathbf{x}|]+1.

From the proof of Proposition
4.2
, we know that

(4.51)

\left(|{\rm I}|+|{\rm II}_{1}|+|{\rm III}|\right)(\mathbf{x})|\leq C(1+|\mathbf{x}|)^{4\alpha-1},

where
{\rm II}_{1}
comes from the following decomposition

\displaystyle{\rm II}=

\displaystyle\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\nabla O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{\alpha}-2}\cdot\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}\mathbf{f}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{4}}(0)}O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\operatorname{div}\left(\phi^{c}_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}\mathbf{f}_{i}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{1}+{\rm II}_{2,1}+{\rm II}_{2,2}

with
\phi^{c}_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}=1-\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}.

So our task is now to bound
{\rm II}_{2,1}
and
{\rm II}_{2,2}
. To begin with, we estimate
{\rm II}_{2,1}
. In terms of estimates (
3.27
), (
3.28
) and (
4.50
), it is easy to check that there exist a positive constant
C
such that

(4.52)

\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\mathbf{f}_{1}|(\mathbf{x})+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{1}|(\mathbf{x})\leq C.

With the decay estimate (
4.52
) in hand, by using the triangle inequality, we readily have that for all
|\mathbf{x}|\geq 200,

|{\rm II}_{2,1}|\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha}}\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}|O_{\alpha,q}|(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\left||\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha}\operatorname{div}\left(\phi^{c}_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}\mathbf{f}_{1}\right)\right|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s.

By the Young inequality and (
4.52
), we obtain

(4.53)

\begin{split}|{\rm II}_{2,1}|\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha}}\bigg(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}|\mathbf{f}_{1}|(\mathbf{x})+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{1}|(\mathbf{x})\bigg)\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\leq&\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha}}\log\langle\mathbf{x}\rangle.\end{split}

Collecting (
4.51
) and (
4.53
) yields that for all
|\mathbf{x}|\geq 200,

(4.54)

\left|\mathbf{V}^{\natural}\right|(\mathbf{x})\leq C(1+|\mathbf{x}|)^{4\alpha-1}.

It remains for us to bound
{\rm II}_{2,2}
for large
|\mathbf{x}|
. Firstly, we note that

(4.55)

\begin{split}{\rm II}_{2,2}=&\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}\int_{0}^{1}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}O_{\alpha,q}(1-s,\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\widetilde{\dot{\Delta}}_{q}\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}^{\sharp}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\,{\rm d}\mathbf{y}{\rm d}s\\
\leq&C\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}2^{-q\gamma}\int_{0}^{1}s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}-2}\|O_{\alpha}\|_{L^{1}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}^{\sharp}\right)(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right\|_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}{\rm d}s,\end{split}

where

\mathbf{f}_{2}^{\sharp}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})=\left(1-\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})\right)\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}).

Since

\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}^{\sharp}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})=-\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})+\left(1-\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})\right)\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\right)(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}),

we further obtain by the fact the Hölder space
\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})=\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})
for all
\gamma\in(0,1)
that

\displaystyle\left\|\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}^{\sharp}\right)(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right\|_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\cdot)\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\left\|\left(1-\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\cdot)\right)\left(\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\right)(\cdot/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}=:{\rm L_{1}}+{\rm L_{2}}.

According to the definition of Hölder space and the support of
\phi
, we have

\displaystyle{\rm L_{1}}=

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\\
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{z})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{z})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}.

Moreover, one has by the triangle inequality that

\displaystyle{\rm L_{1}}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{z})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{z})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{z})\right)\cdot\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle{\rm L_{1}}\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}|\mathbf{f}_{2}|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})

\displaystyle+\left\|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}=:\mathrm{K}_{1}+\mathrm{K}_{2}.

On one hand, using the property of
\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y}),
one has

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Cs^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{1+\gamma}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\left|\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\right|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}_{2}|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Cs^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left|\mathbf{y}\right|^{4\alpha-2}|\mathbf{f}_{2}|(\mathbf{y}).

On the other hand, we split
\mathrm{K}_{2}
into two parts as follows:

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{2}=

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|\geq|\frac{\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\left\|\nabla\left(\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\cdot)\right)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left|\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}:=\mathrm{K}_{2}^{1}+\mathrm{K}_{2}^{2}.

Obviously, we have

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{2}^{1}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{1+\gamma}}\sup_{\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}{\left|\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{1+\gamma}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)}\frac{1}{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}}\left(|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}\left|\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\right|\right)\leq\frac{Cs^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}\left|\mathbf{f}_{2}\right|(\mathbf{y}).

For the term
\mathrm{K}_{2}^{2},
by the classical mean value theorem of the differential calculus, we know there exist
\theta\in(0,1)
such that

\displaystyle\mathrm{K}_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\left|D\mathbf{f}_{2}\right|(\mathbf{y}+\theta(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{y})/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{\gamma}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{1}{\left|\mathbf{y}+\theta(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{y})\right|^{4\alpha-1}}\left|\mathbf{y}+\theta(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{y})\right|^{4\alpha-1}\left|D\mathbf{f}_{2}\right|(\mathbf{y}+\theta(\mathbf{z}-\mathbf{y})/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{Cs^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left|\mathbf{y}\right|^{4\alpha-1}\left|D\mathbf{f}_{2}\right|(\mathbf{y}).

Collecting estimates for
\mathrm{K}_{1}
,
\mathrm{K}_{2}^{1}
and
\mathrm{K}_{2}^{2}
, we readily have

(4.56)

{\rm L_{1}}\leq\frac{Cs^{\frac{2\alpha-1}{\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\bigg(\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2}\left|\mathbf{f}_{2}\right|(\mathbf{y})+\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left|\mathbf{y}\right|^{4\alpha-1}\left|D\mathbf{f}_{2}\right|(\mathbf{y})\bigg).

Next, we deal with the term
{\rm L}_{2}.
By the triangle inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle{\rm L}_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}^{c}(\mathbf{y})-\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}^{c}(\mathbf{z})\big|\,|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}^{c}(\mathbf{z})|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\big|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}=:{\rm L}_{2}^{1}+{\rm L}_{2}^{2}.

Since

\|\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\|\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}\|^{1-\gamma}_{L^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\|\nabla\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}\|^{\gamma}_{L^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{\gamma}},

we have

(4.57)

\begin{split}{\rm L}_{2}^{1}=&\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
\mathbf{y}\neq\mathbf{z}\end{subarray}}\frac{\big|\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{y})-\phi_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(\mathbf{z})\big|\,|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}\\
\leq&\frac{Cs^{\frac{4\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left|\mathbf{y}\right|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}|(\mathbf{y}).\end{split}

As for the last term
{\rm L}_{2}^{2}
, we note that

\displaystyle{\rm L}_{2}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|\geq\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\big|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\big|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}=:{\rm L}_{2}^{2,1}+{\rm L}_{2}^{2,2}.

In the same way as in the proof of
{\rm K}_{2}^{1},
it is obvious

(4.58)

{\rm L}_{2}^{2,1}\leq\frac{Cs^{\frac{4\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left|\mathbf{y}\right|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}|(\mathbf{y}).

For the term
{\rm L}_{2}^{2,2}
, we observe that

\displaystyle{\rm L}_{2}^{2,2}\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\big|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

By the triangle inequality, we have

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\big|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{\big||\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-|\mathbf{z}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\big|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle+\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left||\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}-|\mathbf{z}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\right|\big|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})-\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{z}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\big|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}

\displaystyle=:

\displaystyle{\rm A}+{\rm B}.

According to the definition of the Hölder space, we find that

\displaystyle{\rm A}\leq Cs^{\frac{4\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}\left\||\cdot|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.

On the other hand, the symmetry of
{\rm B}
helps us to conclude

\displaystyle{\rm B}\leq

\displaystyle 2\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\frac{\left||\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}-|\mathbf{z}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\right|}{|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{\gamma}}\big|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}\max\{|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-2+\gamma},|\mathbf{z}|^{4\alpha-2+\gamma}\}|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|^{1-\gamma}\big|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{\begin{subarray}{c}\mathbf{y},\,\mathbf{z}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}\backslash B_{\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{2}}(0)\\
|\mathbf{y}-\mathbf{z}|<\frac{|\mathbf{x}|}{100}\end{subarray}}|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}\big|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\big|(\mathbf{y}/s^{\frac{1}{2\alpha}})\leq Cs^{\frac{4\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}|(\mathbf{y}).

Collecting both estimates for
\rm A
and
\rm B
, we have

{\rm L}_{2}^{2,2}\leq\frac{Cs^{\frac{4\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\bigg(\left\||\cdot|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}|(\mathbf{y})\bigg).

This estimate together with (
4.57
) and (
4.58
) gives

(4.59)

{\rm L}_{2}\leq\frac{Cs^{\frac{4\alpha-1}{2\alpha}}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\bigg(\left\||\cdot|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{C}^{\gamma}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\sup_{\mathbf{y}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-1}|\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}|(\mathbf{y})\bigg).

Inserting (
4.56
) and (
4.59
) into (
4.55
) and then using the conditions (
4.47
) and (
4.49
), we eventually obtain

\displaystyle{\rm II}_{2,2}\leq

\displaystyle C\frac{1}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}\sum_{N_{\mathbf{x}}\leq q<0}2^{-q\gamma}\int_{0}^{1}\left(1+s^{-\frac{1}{2\alpha}}\right){\rm d}s

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}}2^{-\gamma N_{\mathbf{x}}}\leq\frac{C}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}}.

Thus, we complete the proof of Proposition
4.3
.
∎

With Proposition
4.3
in hand, we turn to show the optimal estimate in Theorem
1.8
. To do this, we just need to prove (
4.47
) and (
4.49
). Since
\sigma\in C^{1,0}(\mathbb{S}^{2})
and
\mathbf{f}_{2}=\Div(\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0})
, we have in terms of Proposition 4.1 established in
[
11
]
that

(4.60)

\begin{split}&\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-2}\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x})\right|+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}\left||\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1}\nabla\mathbf{f}_{2}(\mathbf{x})\right|\\
\leq&C\bigg(\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{2\alpha-1}|\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})\bigg)^{2}+\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{2\alpha-1}|\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{2\alpha}|\Div\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})<\infty.\end{split}

Thus, our task is now to show

(4.61)

\left\||\cdot|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\operatorname{div}\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty.

In view of the Bony paraproduct, we write

|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-1+\gamma}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}=\dot{T}_{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}}|\mathbf{x}|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}+\dot{T}_{|\mathbf{x}|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}}{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}}+\dot{R}\left(|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma},|\mathbf{x}|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right).

In terms of the property of support, we can show by using
\alpha\in[5/6,1]
and
\gamma\in(0,1)
that

\displaystyle\left\|\dot{T}_{|\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}}{|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{q\gamma}\left\|\dot{S}_{q-1}\big(|\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\big)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\dot{\Delta}_{q}\big(|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\big)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left\||\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{3-4\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\||\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\left\||\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{3-4\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})},

and

\displaystyle\left\|\dot{R}\left(|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma},|\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{q\in\mathbb{Z}}2^{q\gamma}\left\|\dot{\Delta}_{q}\big(|\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\big)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\|\widetilde{\dot{\Delta}}_{q}\big(|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\big)\right\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\left\||\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{3-4\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\left\||\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\leq C\left\||\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{3-4\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.

Since
\mathbf{f}_{2}=-\mathbf{U}_{0}\otimes\mathbf{U}_{0}
and
\sigma\in C^{1,0}(\mathbb{S}^{2}),
we have by (
2.2
) that

(4.62)

\begin{split}&\left\||\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{3-4\alpha}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\\
\leq&C\left\||\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right\|_{L^{\frac{3}{4\alpha-3},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}\\
\leq&C\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{2\alpha-1}|\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})\sup_{\mathbf{x}\in\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}|^{2\alpha}|\Div\mathbf{U}_{0}|(\mathbf{x})\left\||\cdot|^{3-4\alpha}\right\|_{L^{\frac{3}{4\alpha-3},\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}.\end{split}

This estimate together with Proposition 4.1 established in
[
11
]
means that

(4.63)

\left\|\dot{T}_{|\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}}{|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}}\right\|_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}+\left\|\dot{R}\left(|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma},|\cdot|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}\right)\right\|_{\dot{B}^{\gamma}_{\infty,\infty}(\mathbf{R}^{3})}<\infty.

Next, we turn to bound the paraproduct term
\dot{T}_{|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}}|\mathbf{x}|^{2}\Div\mathbf{f}_{2}.
Since

\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}h_{q}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}=\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}h(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}=\hat{h}(0)=1\quad\text{for each}\,\,q\in\mathbb{Z},

we have

\displaystyle\dot{S}_{q-1}\big(|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\left(\dot{S}_{q-1}|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}-2^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\right)+2^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}h_{q}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}-2^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}+2^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}.

Performing the fact
(a+b)^{p}\leq 2^{p}(a^{p}+b^{p})
with
p\geq 0,
one has

\displaystyle\left|\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}h_{q}(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\left(|\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}-2^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\right)\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\right|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\int_{\mathbf{R}^{3}}|\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}|h_{q}|(\mathbf{x}-\mathbf{y})\,\mathrm{d}\mathbf{y}\leq C2^{-q(4\alpha-3+\gamma)}.

Thus, we have

\left|\dot{S}_{q-1}\big(|\cdot|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}\big)\right|\leq C2^{-q(4\alpha-3+\gamma)}+2^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}|\mathbf{x}|^{4\alpha-3+\gamma}.

With the inequality in hand, we can show by (
2.2
) that
